I don't know why, but sound engineers always seem to wear Hawaiian shirts or cargo shorts. It's like a uniform. My uncle used to wear cargo shorts all the time. He said he needed the pockets for all his tools, but he mostly just kept peppermints and golf tees in them. He loved golf. He'd go out every Sunday afternoon, even if it was raining. He said the rain made the greens softer, which helped his short game. I went with him once, but I'm terrible at golf. I always slice the ball into the woods...\n\nBut anyway, expecting bad things? I don't know. I just... I don't really know.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself being overly aware of your surroundings, even when there's no apparent threat?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I mean, I can't even sit with my back to a door anymore. It's just... I have to see who's coming in. And when I'm at church events now, I'm not even paying attention to the people in front of me. I'm just always watching hands, looking at corners, noticing if a car slows down outside. I'm constantly scoping out where the nearest exit is. We actually have four exits in the main sanctuary, which is good for fire code. The fire marshal came by last month to do an inspection. He was this really gruff guy named Steve. He made us change out all the batteries in the emergency exit signs because they were like, a week past their expiration date. We had to buy these special 6-volt batteries that you can't just get at a regular grocery store. I had to drive all the way out to the industrial park to this specialty battery warehouse. It's this massive building, just aisles and aisles of batteries. They even had those huge ones for forklifts. Forklifts are so dangerous, honestly. My buddy Dave used to drive a forklift at a warehouse, and he said guys would always try to do tricks on them, like popping wheelies. Which is insane because those things weigh thousands of pounds. If one tips over on you, you're done. He said they had to watch these super cheesy safety videos from the 90s where the actors would dramatically overreact to getting their toes run over... \n\nBut yeah, I'm just always watching. Always looking for the exits, watching people's hands. It's exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself being easily irritated or angered by small things that wouldn't have bothered you before your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I... I really have been. It's bad. I've snapped in ministry meetings over things that shouldn't even matter. Just last week, I raised my voice at the deacons and a couple of the youth leaders over the scheduling for the fall retreat. I was talking to them like they were the enemy, you know? Just completely went off on them. And afterward, I just hate how fast I went there. I never used to be like that. The fall retreat is usually my favorite thing to plan. We go up to this camp in the mountains, Camp Cedar Ridge. It's beautiful up there in October when the leaves are changing. The camp director, his name is Pastor Mike, he's been running the place for like thirty years. He has this massive golden retriever named Samson who just wanders around the camp all day begging for hot dogs. The kids love him. We always do a big bonfire on the last night, and we make s'mores. But you have to be careful with the marshmallows because if you get them too close to the coals, they just turn into a fireball. My little sister used to do that on purpose when we were kids. She'd catch her marshmallow on fire and then wave it around like a torch until my dad yelled at her. He was always so strict about fire safety. He was a volunteer firefighter for a while in our hometown. They had this old siren on top of the firehouse that would go off whenever there was a call, and it was so loud you could hear it from miles away. It sounded like an air raid siren from World War II. I used to watch all these documentaries on the History Channel about World War II with my grandpa...\n\nBut yeah, the anger... I just snap at people now. I don't mean to, but it just happens so fast.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself constantly looking over your shoulder or checking your surroundings, even when you're in a safe place?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I just mentioned that, actually. It's like I was saying, I really can't even sit with my back to a door anymore. It's impossible. And when I'm at church events, I'm not even paying attention to the people I'm supposed to be talking to. I'm just constantly watching people's hands, checking the corners of the room, noticing if a car slows down outside the building... I'm always scoping out where the nearest exit is. Which is why I know we have four exits in the sanctuary. And like I said, the fire marshal, Steve, he was super strict about those exit signs. He actually told us this crazy story about a church he inspected a few years ago where they had chained the back doors shut from the inside to keep people from sneaking in late during the service. Can you imagine? If there had been a fire, it would have been a complete disaster. It's actually illegal to do that, it's a huge fire code violation. He said he threatened to shut the whole place down right then and there. I mean, you have to take that stuff seriously. Fire spreads so fast, especially in older buildings. Our church was built in the 70s, so there's a lot of old wood and carpet everywhere. The carpet in the fellowship hall is this hideous orange and brown shag. We've been trying to raise the money to replace it for like three years, but the building fund is always getting drained by other stuff, like the HVAC system. The AC unit on the roof broke down in the middle of July last year, and it was like an oven in there. We had to rent these massive industrial fans just to get through the Sunday service. The fans were so loud you could barely hear the worship band...\n\nBut yeah, the checking things... I just can't stop doing it. I'm always looking for the exits and watching people.\n\nDoctor: Are there any things you used to enjoy doing before your cousin's death that you still find some pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really, honestly. I used to love preaching, playing keys, taking pictures, running with the guys on the court... but now I mostly just go through the motions. Or I just avoid it altogether, to be honest. None of it feels alive in me anymore. \n\nLike, even with photography, I used to take my camera everywhere. I had this really sweet Canon DSLR. Lenses are so expensive, though. I saved up for months to buy this one 50mm prime lens because it gives you that really nice blurry background, you know? The bokeh effect. My friend Sarah is a wedding photographer, and she has like twenty different lenses. She makes really good money doing weddings, but she says it's incredibly stressful because the brides are always freaking out over the tiniest things. Like, one time she shot a wedding where the florist brought the wrong shade of pink roses, and the bride literally locked herself in the bathroom for two hours. They had to push the whole ceremony back. And the caterer was getting so mad because the chicken was drying out under the heat lamps. Catering is a tough business. You have to cook for like two hundred people and keep it all warm. I tried to cook a turkey for Friendsgiving once and totally messed it up. Left the little bag of organs inside it and everything. My mom laughed so hard she cried when I told her. She makes the best turkey, she brines it for like two days in this huge bucket in the garage fridge... \n\nBut yeah, I don't know. Preaching, playing keys, basketball... none of it feels alive. I just go through the motions now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think that you're a bad person or that you're worthless since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: No, I don't... I don't really have those thoughts. I mean, I don't think I'm a bad person or worthless or anything like that. I actually preached a whole sermon series on our worth and value a few months ago. We were going through the book of Ephesians. It's probably my favorite book to teach from, honestly. Paul wrote it when he was in prison, which is just crazy to think about. Roman prisons weren't like modern jails with beds and meals, they were basically just dark, damp holes in the ground. \n\nI actually got to visit Rome on a study abroad trip in college. We went to the Mamertine Prison where they think he was held. The whole city is just incredible, though. The line for the Colosseum was like three hours long, and it was so incredibly hot that day. There were all these guys walking around the line selling tiny, warm bottles of water for like five euros. Total rip-off, but people were buying them because they were just desperate. We ended up leaving the line and just getting gelato instead. I had this pistachio gelato near the Trevi Fountain that literally changed my life. I actually bought an ice cream maker when I got back to the States because I wanted to try making it myself. It was one of those machines where you have to freeze the inner bowl for like twenty-four hours before you use it. But I totally forgot to freeze it, so I just poured the heavy cream and sugar in and it never set up. It just turned into this weird, sweet milk soup. My roommate Dave drank it anyway, though. Dave would literally consume anything. He once ate a slice of pepperoni pizza that had been sitting out on our kitchen counter for three days. He had an iron stomach. He works in finance now, out in Chicago. He's always trying to get me to invest in crypto, but I just don't understand how any of that digital money stuff actually works...\n\nBut yeah, to answer your question... no, I don't think I'm a bad person. That's not really something I've been feeling."
  }
]
